Method code:
139 |   private void discoverBackEdges(DiGraphNode<N, E> u) {
140 |     u.setAnnotation(GRAY);
141 |     for (DiGraphEdge<N, E> e : u.getOutEdges()) {
142 |       if (ignoreEdge(e)) {
143 |         continue;
144 |       }
145 |       DiGraphNode<N, E> v = e.getDestination();
146 |       if (v.getAnnotation() == WHITE) {
147 |         discoverBackEdges(v);
148 |       } else if (v.getAnnotation() == GRAY) {
149 |         e.setAnnotation(BACK_EDGE);
150 |       }
151 |     }
152 |     u.setAnnotation(BLACK);
153 |   }

372 |   private void handleWhile(Node node) {
373 |     // Control goes to the first statement if the condition evaluates to true.
374 |     createEdge(node, Branch.ON_TRUE,
375 |         computeFallThrough(node.getFirstChild().getNext()));
376 | 
377 |     // Control goes to the follow() if the condition evaluates to false.
378 |     createEdge(node, Branch.ON_FALSE,
379 |         computeFollowNode(node, this));

381 |         node, NodeUtil.getConditionExpression(node));

===== M0010 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.FunctionParamBuilder.addVarArgs(JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/FunctionParamBuilder.java
Original range: 94-109
Documentation:
/**
   * Add variable arguments to the end of the parameter list.
   * @return False if this is called after var args are added.
   */
Method code:
94 |   public boolean addVarArgs(JSType type) {
95 |     if (hasVarArgs()) {
96 |       return false;
97 |     }
98 | 
99 |     // There are two types of variable argument functions:
100 |     // 1) Programmer-defined var args
101 |     // 2) Native bottom types that can accept any argument.
102 |     // For the first one, "undefined" is a valid value for all arguments.
103 |     // For the second, we do not want to cast it up to undefined.
104 |     if (!type.isEmptyType()) {
105 |       type = registry.createOptionalType(type);
106 |     }
107 |     newParameter(type).setVarArgs(true);
108 |     return true;
109 |   }

===== M0015 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.FunctionType.setPrototypeBasedOn(ObjectType,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/FunctionType.java
Original range: 345-371
Documentation:

Method code:
345 |   void setPrototypeBasedOn(ObjectType baseType, Node propertyNode) {
346 |     // This is a bit weird. We need to successfully handle these
347 |     // two cases:
348 |     // Foo.prototype = new Bar();
349 |     // and
350 |     // Foo.prototype = {baz: 3};
351 |     // In the first case, we do not want new properties to get
352 |     // added to Bar. In the second case, we do want new properties
353 |     // to get added to the type of the anonymous object.
354 |     //
355 |     // We handle this by breaking it into two cases:
356 |     //
357 |     // In the first case, we create a new PrototypeObjectType and set
358 |     // its implicit prototype to the type being assigned. This ensures
359 |     // that Bar will not get any properties of Foo.prototype, but properties
360 |     // later assigned to Bar will get inherited properly.
361 |     //
362 |     // In the second case, we just use the anonymous object as the prototype.
363 |     if (baseType.hasReferenceName() ||
364 |         isNativeObjectType() ||
365 |         baseType.isFunctionPrototypeType()) {
366 | 
367 |       baseType = new PrototypeObjectType(
368 |           registry, this.getReferenceName() + ".prototype", baseType);
369 |     }
370 |     setPrototype(baseType, propertyNode);
371 |   }

Original range: 162-173
Documentation:

Method code:
162 |   @Override
163 |   public boolean shouldTraverse(NodeTraversal t, Node n, Node parent) {
164 |     for (CallbackWrapper callback : callbacks) {
165 |       callback.shouldTraverseIfActive(t, n, parent);
166 |     }
167 |     // Note that this method could return false if all callbacks are inactive.
168 |     // This apparent optimization would make this method more expensive
169 |     // in the typical case where not all nodes are inactive. It is
170 |     // very unlikely that many all callbacks would be inactive at the same
171 |     // time (indeed, there are several checking passes that never return false).
172 |     return true;
173 |   }

===== M0027 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeCheck.ensureTyped(NodeTraversal,Node,JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeCheck.java
Original range: 1883-1907
Documentation:
/**
   * Enforces type casts, and ensures the node is typed.
   *
   * A cast in the way that we use it in JSDoc annotations never
   * alters the generated code and therefore never can induce any runtime
   * operation. What this means is that a 'cast' is really just a compile
   * time constraint on the underlying value. In the future, we may add
   * support for run-time casts for compiled tests.
   *
   * To ensure some shred of sanity, we enforce the notion that the
   * type you are casting to may only meaningfully be a narrower type
   * than the underlying declared type. We also invalidate optimizations
   * on bad type casts.
   *
   * @param t The traversal object needed to report errors.
   * @param n The node getting a type assigned to it.
   * @param type The type to be assigned.
   */
Method code:
1883 |   private void ensureTyped(NodeTraversal t, Node n, JSType type) {
1884 |     // Make sure FUNCTION nodes always get function type.
1885 |     Preconditions.checkState(!n.isFunction() ||
1886 |             type.isFunctionType() ||
1887 |             type.isUnknownType());
1888 |     JSDocInfo info = n.getJSDocInfo();
1889 |     if (info != null) {
1890 |       if (info.hasType()) {
1891 |         JSType infoType = info.getType().evaluate(t.getScope(), typeRegistry);
1892 |         validator.expectCanCast(t, n, infoType, type);
1893 |         type = infoType;
1894 |       }
1895 | 
1896 |       if (info.isImplicitCast() && !inExterns) {
1897 |         String propName = n.isGetProp() ?
1898 |             n.getLastChild().getString() : "(missing)";
1899 |         compiler.report(
1900 |             t.makeError(n, ILLEGAL_IMPLICIT_CAST, propName));
1901 |       }
1902 |     }
1903 | 
1904 |     if (n.getJSType() == null) {
1905 |       n.setJSType(type);
1906 |     }
1907 |   }

===== M0034 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypedScopeCreator.createScope(Node,Scope)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypedScopeCreator.java
Original range: 191-240
Documentation:
/**
   * Creates a scope with all types declared. Declares newly discovered types
   * and type properties in the type registry.
   */
Method code:
191 |   @Override
192 |   public Scope createScope(Node root, Scope parent) {
193 |     // Constructing the global scope is very different than constructing
194 |     // inner scopes, because only global scopes can contain named classes that
195 |     // show up in the type registry.
196 |     Scope newScope = null;
197 |     AbstractScopeBuilder scopeBuilder = null;
198 |     if (parent == null) {
199 |       // Run a first-order analysis over the syntax tree.
200 |       (new FirstOrderFunctionAnalyzer(compiler, functionAnalysisResults))
201 |           .process(root.getFirstChild(), root.getLastChild());
202 | 
203 |       // Find all the classes in the global scope.
204 |       newScope = createInitialScope(root);
205 | 
206 |       GlobalScopeBuilder globalScopeBuilder = new GlobalScopeBuilder(newScope);
207 |       scopeBuilder = globalScopeBuilder;
208 |       NodeTraversal.traverse(compiler, root, scopeBuilder);
209 |     } else {
210 |       newScope = new Scope(parent, root);
211 |       LocalScopeBuilder localScopeBuilder = new LocalScopeBuilder(newScope);
212 |       scopeBuilder = localScopeBuilder;
213 |       localScopeBuilder.build();
214 |     }
215 | 
216 |     scopeBuilder.resolveStubDeclarations();
217 |     scopeBuilder.resolveTypes();
218 | 
219 |     // Gather the properties in each function that we found in the
220 |     // global scope, if that function has a @this type that we can
221 |     // build properties on.
222 |     for (Node functionNode : scopeBuilder.nonExternFunctions) {
223 |       JSType type = functionNode.getJSType();
224 |       if (type != null && type.isFunctionType()) {
225 |         FunctionType fnType = type.toMaybeFunctionType();
226 |         ObjectType fnThisType = fnType.getTypeOfThis();
227 |         if (!fnThisType.isUnknownType()) {
228 |           NodeTraversal.traverse(compiler, functionNode.getLastChild(),
229 |               scopeBuilder.new CollectProperties(fnThisType));
230 |         }
231 |       }
232 |     }
233 | 
234 |     if (parent == null) {
235 |       codingConvention.defineDelegateProxyPrototypeProperties(
236 |           typeRegistry, newScope, delegateProxyPrototypes,
237 |           delegateCallingConventions);
238 |     }
239 |     return newScope;
240 |   }

===== M0038 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.JsDocInfoParser.parseTopLevelTypeExpression(JsDocToken)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/parsing/JsDocInfoParser.java
Original range: 1637-1653
Documentation:
/**
   * TopLevelTypeExpression := TypeExpression
   *     | TypeUnionList
   *
   * We made this rule up, for the sake of backwards compatibility.
   */
Method code:
1637 |   private Node parseTopLevelTypeExpression(JsDocToken token) {
1638 |     Node typeExpr = parseTypeExpression(token);
1639 |     if (typeExpr != null) {
1640 |       // top-level unions are allowed
1641 |       if (match(JsDocToken.PIPE)) {
1642 |         next();
1643 |         if (match(JsDocToken.PIPE)) {
1644 |           // We support double pipes for backwards-compatibility.
1645 |           next();
1646 |         }
1647 |         skipEOLs();
1648 |         token = next();
1649 |         return parseUnionTypeWithAlternate(token, typeExpr);
1650 |       }
1651 |     }
1652 |     return typeExpr;
1653 |   }

===== M0042 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeCheck.checkNoTypeCheckSection(Node,boolean)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeCheck.java
Original range: 397-415
Documentation:

Method code:
397 |   private void checkNoTypeCheckSection(Node n, boolean enterSection) {
398 |     switch (n.getType()) {
399 |       case Token.SCRIPT:
400 |       case Token.BLOCK:
401 |       case Token.VAR:
402 |       case Token.FUNCTION:
403 |       case Token.ASSIGN:
404 |         JSDocInfo info = n.getJSDocInfo();
405 |         if (info != null && info.isNoTypeCheck()) {
406 |           if (enterSection) {
407 |             noTypeCheckSection++;
408 |           } else {
409 |             noTypeCheckSection--;
410 |           }
411 |         }
412 |         validator.setShouldReport(noTypeCheckSection == 0);
413 |         break;
414 |     }

Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.FunctionType.resolveInternal(ErrorReporter,StaticScope<JSType>)

Original range: 1107-1156
Documentation:

Method code:
1107 |   @Override
1108 |   JSType resolveInternal(ErrorReporter t, StaticScope<JSType> scope) {
1109 |     setResolvedTypeInternal(this);
1110 | 
1111 |     call = (ArrowType) safeResolve(call, t, scope);
1112 |     if (prototypeSlot != null) {
1113 |       prototypeSlot.setType(
1114 |           safeResolve(prototypeSlot.getType(), t, scope));
1115 |     }
1116 | 
1117 |     // Warning about typeOfThis if it doesn't resolve to an ObjectType
1118 |     // is handled further upstream.
1119 |     //
1120 |     // TODO(nicksantos): Handle this correctly if we have a UnionType.
1121 |     //
1122 |     // TODO(nicksantos): In ES3, the run-time coerces "null" to the global
1123 |     // activation object. In ES5, it leaves it as null. Just punt on this
1124 |     // issue for now by coercing out null. This is complicated by the
1125 |     // fact that when most people write @this {Foo}, they really don't
1126 |     // mean "nullable Foo". For certain tags (like @extends) we de-nullify
1127 |     // the name for them.
1128 |     JSType maybeTypeOfThis = safeResolve(typeOfThis, t, scope);
1129 |     if (maybeTypeOfThis != null) {
1130 |       maybeTypeOfThis = maybeTypeOfThis.restrictByNotNullOrUndefined();
1131 |     }
1132 |     if (maybeTypeOfThis instanceof ObjectType) {
1133 |       typeOfThis = (ObjectType) maybeTypeOfThis;
1134 |     }
1135 | 
1136 |     boolean changed = false;
1137 |     ImmutableList.Builder<ObjectType> resolvedInterfaces =
1138 |         ImmutableList.builder();
1139 |     for (ObjectType iface : implementedInterfaces) {
1140 |       ObjectType resolvedIface = (ObjectType) iface.resolve(t, scope);
1141 |       resolvedInterfaces.add(resolvedIface);
1142 |       changed |= (resolvedIface != iface);
1143 |     }
1144 |     if (changed) {
1145 |       implementedInterfaces = resolvedInterfaces.build();
1146 |     }
1147 | 
1148 |     if (subTypes != null) {
1149 |       for (int i = 0; i < subTypes.size(); i++) {
1150 |         subTypes.set(
1151 |             i, JSType.toMaybeFunctionType(subTypes.get(i).resolve(t, scope)));
1152 |       }
1153 |     }
1154 | 
1155 |     return super.resolveInternal(t, scope);
1156 |   }

===== M0077 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.ObjectType.isUnknownType()
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/ObjectType.java
Original range: 504-524
Documentation:
/**
   * We treat this as the unknown type if any of its implicit prototype
   * properties is unknown.
   */
Method code:
504 |   @Override
505 |   public boolean isUnknownType() {
506 |     // If the object is unknown now, check the supertype again,
507 |     // because it might have been resolved since the last check.
508 |     if (unknown) {
509 |       ObjectType implicitProto = getImplicitPrototype();
510 |       if (implicitProto == null ||
511 |           implicitProto.isNativeObjectType()) {
512 |         unknown = false;
513 |         for (ObjectType interfaceType : getCtorExtendedInterfaces()) {
514 |           if (interfaceType.isUnknownType()) {
515 |             unknown = true;
516 |             break;
517 |           }
518 |         }
519 |       } else {
520 |         unknown = implicitProto.isUnknownType();
521 |       }
522 |     }
523 |     return unknown;
524 |   }

===== M0078 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.Compiler.parseInputs()
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/Compiler.java
Original range: 1240-1362
Documentation:
/**
   * Parses the externs and main inputs.
   *
   * @return A synthetic root node whose two children are the externs root
   *     and the main root
   */
Method code:
1240 |   Node parseInputs() {
1241 |     boolean devMode = options.devMode != DevMode.OFF;
1242 | 
1243 |     // If old roots exist (we are parsing a second time), detach each of the
1244 |     // individual file parse trees.
1245 |     if (externsRoot != null) {
1246 |       externsRoot.detachChildren();
1247 |     }
1248 |     if (jsRoot != null) {
1249 |       jsRoot.detachChildren();
1250 |     }
1251 | 
1252 |     // Parse main JS sources.
1253 |     jsRoot = IR.block();
1254 |     jsRoot.setIsSyntheticBlock(true);
1255 | 
1256 |     externsRoot = IR.block();
1257 |     externsRoot.setIsSyntheticBlock(true);
1258 | 
1259 |     externAndJsRoot = IR.block(externsRoot, jsRoot);
1260 |     externAndJsRoot.setIsSyntheticBlock(true);
1261 | 
1262 |     if (options.tracer.isOn()) {
1263 |       tracker = new PerformanceTracker(jsRoot, options.tracer);
1264 |       addChangeHandler(tracker.getCodeChangeHandler());
1265 |     }
1266 | 
1267 |     Tracer tracer = newTracer("parseInputs");
1268 | 
1269 |     try {
1270 |       // Parse externs sources.
1271 |       for (CompilerInput input : externs) {
1272 |         Node n = input.getAstRoot(this);
1273 |         if (hasErrors()) {
1274 |           return null;
1275 |         }
1276 |         externsRoot.addChildToBack(n);
1277 |       }
1278 | 
1279 |       // Modules inferred in ProcessCommonJS pass.
1280 |       if (options.transformAMDToCJSModules || options.processCommonJSModules) {
1281 |         processAMDAndCommonJSModules();
1282 |       }
1283 | 
1284 |       hoistExterns(externsRoot);
1285 | 
1286 |       // Check if the sources need to be re-ordered.
1287 |       boolean staleInputs = false;
1288 |       if (options.dependencyOptions.needsManagement()) {
1289 |         for (CompilerInput input : inputs) {
1290 |           // Forward-declare all the provided types, so that they
1291 |           // are not flagged even if they are dropped from the process.
1292 |           for (String provide : input.getProvides()) {
1293 |             getTypeRegistry().forwardDeclareType(provide);
1294 |           }
1295 |         }
1296 | 
1297 |         try {
1298 |           inputs =
1299 |               (moduleGraph == null ? new JSModuleGraph(modules) : moduleGraph)
1300 |               .manageDependencies(options.dependencyOptions, inputs);
1301 |           staleInputs = true;
1302 |         } catch (CircularDependencyException e) {
1303 |           report(JSError.make(
1304 |               JSModule.CIRCULAR_DEPENDENCY_ERROR, e.getMessage()));
1305 | 
1306 |           // If in IDE mode, we ignore the error and keep going.
1307 |           if (hasErrors()) {
1308 |             return null;
1309 |           }
1310 |         } catch (MissingProvideException e) {
1311 |           report(JSError.make(
1312 |               MISSING_ENTRY_ERROR, e.getMessage()));
1313 | 
1314 |           // If in IDE mode, we ignore the error and keep going.
1315 |           if (hasErrors()) {
1316 |             return null;
1317 |           }
1318 |         }
1319 |       }
1320 | 
1321 |       hoistNoCompileFiles();
1322 | 
1323 |       if (staleInputs) {
1324 |         repartitionInputs();
1325 |       }
1326 | 
1327 |       // Build the AST.
1328 |       for (CompilerInput input : inputs) {
1329 |         Node n = input.getAstRoot(this);
1330 |         if (n == null) {
1331 |           continue;
1332 |         }
1333 | 
1334 |         if (devMode) {
1335 |           runSanityCheck();
1336 |           if (hasErrors()) {
1337 |             return null;
1338 |           }
1339 |         }
1340 | 
1341 |         if (options.sourceMapOutputPath != null ||
1342 |             options.nameReferenceReportPath != null) {
1343 | 
1344 |           // Annotate the nodes in the tree with information from the
1345 |           // input file. This information is used to construct the SourceMap.
1346 |           SourceInformationAnnotator sia =
1347 |               new SourceInformationAnnotator(
1348 |                   input.getName(), options.devMode != DevMode.OFF);
1349 |           NodeTraversal.traverse(this, n, sia);
1350 |         }
1351 | 
1352 |         jsRoot.addChildToBack(n);
1353 |       }
1354 | 
1355 |       if (hasErrors()) {
1356 |         return null;
1357 |       }
1358 |       return externAndJsRoot;
1359 |     } finally {
1360 |       stopTracer(tracer, "parseInputs");
1361 |     }
1362 |   }

===== M0096 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.BasicErrorManager.LeveledJSErrorComparator.compare(ErrorWithLevel,ErrorWithLevel)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/BasicErrorManager.java
Original range: 128-179
Documentation:

Method code:
128 |     @Override
129 |     public int compare(ErrorWithLevel p1, ErrorWithLevel p2) {
130 |       // null is the smallest value
131 |       if (p2 == null) {
132 |         if (p1 == null) {
133 |           return 0;
134 |         } else {
135 |           return P1_GT_P2;
136 |         }
137 |       }
138 | 
139 |       // check level
140 |       if (p1.level != p2.level) {
141 |         return p2.level.compareTo(p1.level);
142 |       }
143 | 
144 |       // sourceName comparison
145 |       String source1 = p1.error.sourceName;
146 |       String source2 = p2.error.sourceName;
147 |       if (source1 != null && source2 != null) {
148 |         int sourceCompare = source1.compareTo(source2);
149 |         if (sourceCompare != 0) {
150 |           return sourceCompare;
151 |         }
152 |       } else if (source1 == null && source2 != null) {
153 |         return P1_LT_P2;
154 |       } else if (source1 != null && source2 == null) {
155 |         return P1_GT_P2;
156 |       }
157 |       // lineno comparison
158 |       int lineno1 = p1.error.lineNumber;
159 |       int lineno2 = p2.error.lineNumber;
160 |       if (lineno1 != lineno2) {
161 |         return lineno1 - lineno2;
162 |       } else if (lineno1 < 0 && 0 <= lineno2) {
163 |         return P1_LT_P2;
164 |       } else if (0 <= lineno1 && lineno2 < 0) {
165 |         return P1_GT_P2;
166 |       }
167 |       // charno comparison
168 |       int charno1 = p1.error.getCharno();
169 |       int charno2 = p2.error.getCharno();
170 |       if (charno1 != charno2) {
171 |         return charno1 - charno2;
172 |       } else if (charno1 < 0 && 0 <= charno2) {
173 |         return P1_LT_P2;
174 |       } else if (0 <= charno1 && charno2 < 0) {
175 |         return P1_GT_P2;
176 |       }
177 |       // description
178 |       return p1.error.description.compareTo(p2.error.description);
179 |     }

===== M0107 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.shouldTraverse(NodeTraversal,Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 217-292
Documentation:

Method code:
217 |   @Override
218 |   public boolean shouldTraverse(
219 |       NodeTraversal nodeTraversal, Node n, Node parent) {
220 |     astPosition.put(n, astPositionCounter++);
221 | 
222 |     switch (n.getType()) {
223 |       case Token.FUNCTION:
224 |         if (shouldTraverseFunctions || n == cfg.getEntry().getValue()) {
225 |           exceptionHandler.push(n);
226 |           return true;
227 |         }
228 |         return false;
229 |       case Token.TRY:
230 |         exceptionHandler.push(n);
231 |         return true;
232 |     }
233 | 
234 |     /*
235 |      * We are going to stop the traversal depending on what the node's parent
236 |      * is.
237 |      *
238 |      * We are only interested in adding edges between nodes that change control
239 |      * flow. The most obvious ones are loops and IF-ELSE's. A statement
240 |      * transfers control to its next sibling.
241 |      *
242 |      * In case of an expression tree, there is no control flow within the tree
243 |      * even when there are short circuited operators and conditionals. When we
244 |      * are doing data flow analysis, we will simply synthesize lattices up the
245 |      * expression tree by finding the meet at each expression node.
246 |      *
247 |      * For example: within a Token.SWITCH, the expression in question does not
248 |      * change the control flow and need not to be considered.
249 |      */
250 |     if (parent != null) {
251 |       switch (parent.getType()) {
252 |         case Token.FOR:
253 |           // Only traverse the body of the for loop.
254 |           return n == parent.getLastChild();
255 | 
256 |         // Skip the conditions.
257 |         case Token.IF:
258 |         case Token.WHILE:
259 |         case Token.WITH:
260 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild();
261 |         case Token.DO:
262 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild().getNext();
263 |         // Only traverse the body of the cases
264 |         case Token.SWITCH:
265 |         case Token.CASE:
266 |         case Token.CATCH:
267 |         case Token.LABEL:
268 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild();
269 |         case Token.FUNCTION:
270 |           return n == parent.getFirstChild().getNext().getNext();
271 |         case Token.CONTINUE:
272 |         case Token.BREAK:
273 |         case Token.EXPR_RESULT:
274 |         case Token.VAR:
275 |         case Token.RETURN:
276 |         case Token.THROW:
277 |           return false;
278 |         case Token.TRY:
279 |           /* Just before we are about to visit the second child of the TRY node,
280 |            * we know that we will be visiting either the CATCH or the FINALLY.
281 |            * In other words, we know that the post order traversal of the TRY
282 |            * block has been finished, no more exceptions can be caught by the
283 |            * handler at this TRY block and should be taken out of the stack.
284 |            */
285 |           if (n == parent.getFirstChild().getNext()) {
286 |             Preconditions.checkState(exceptionHandler.peek() == parent);
287 |             exceptionHandler.pop();
288 |           }
289 |       }
290 |     }
291 |     return true;
292 |   }

===== M0112 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.JSTypeRegistry.registerPropertyOnType(String,JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/JSTypeRegistry.java
Original range: 619-631
Documentation:
/**
   * Tells the type system that {@code owner} may have a property named
   * {@code propertyName}. This allows the registry to keep track of what
   * types a property is defined upon.
   *
   * This is NOT the same as saying that {@code owner} must have a property
   * named type. ObjectType#hasProperty attempts to minimize false positives
   * ("if we're not sure, then don't type check this property"). The type
   * registry, on the other hand, should attempt to minimize false negatives
   * ("if this property is assigned anywhere in the program, it must
   * show up in the type registry").
   */
Method code:
619 |   public void registerPropertyOnType(String propertyName, JSType type) {
620 |     UnionTypeBuilder typeSet = typesIndexedByProperty.get(propertyName);
621 |     if (typeSet == null) {
622 |       typeSet = new UnionTypeBuilder(this, PROPERTY_CHECKING_UNION_SIZE);
623 |       typesIndexedByProperty.put(propertyName, typeSet);
624 |     }
625 | 
626 |     typeSet.addAlternate(type);
627 |     addReferenceTypeIndexedByProperty(propertyName, type);
628 | 
629 |     // Clear cached values that depend on typesIndexedByProperty.
630 |     greatestSubtypeByProperty.remove(propertyName);
631 |   }

===== M0120 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.type.SemanticReverseAbstractInterpreter.caseEquality(Node,Node,FlowScope,Function<TypePair, TypePair>)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/type/SemanticReverseAbstractInterpreter.java
Original range: 272-311
Documentation:

Method code:
272 |   private FlowScope caseEquality(Node left, Node right, FlowScope blindScope,
273 |       Function<TypePair, TypePair> merging) {
274 |     // left type
275 |     JSType leftType = getTypeIfRefinable(left, blindScope);
276 |     boolean leftIsRefineable;
277 |     if (leftType != null) {
278 |       leftIsRefineable = true;
279 |     } else {
280 |       leftIsRefineable = false;
281 |       leftType = left.getJSType();
282 |     }
283 | 
284 |     // right type
285 |     JSType rightType = getTypeIfRefinable(right, blindScope);
286 |     boolean rightIsRefineable;
287 |     if (rightType != null) {
288 |       rightIsRefineable = true;
289 |     } else {
290 |       rightIsRefineable = false;
291 |       rightType = right.getJSType();
292 |     }
293 | 
294 |     // merged types
295 |     TypePair merged = merging.apply(new TypePair(leftType, rightType));
296 | 
297 |     // creating new scope
298 |     if (merged != null &&
299 |         ((leftIsRefineable && merged.typeA != null) ||
300 |          (rightIsRefineable && merged.typeB != null))) {
301 |       FlowScope informed = blindScope.createChildFlowScope();
302 |       if (leftIsRefineable && merged.typeA != null) {
303 |         declareNameInScope(informed, left, merged.typeA);
304 |       }
305 |       if (rightIsRefineable && merged.typeB != null) {
306 |         declareNameInScope(informed, right, merged.typeB);
307 |       }
308 |       return informed;
309 |     }
310 |     return blindScope;
311 |   }

===== M0142 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeCheck.visitFunction(NodeTraversal,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeCheck.java
Original range: 1523-1584
Documentation:
/**
   * Visits a {@link Token#FUNCTION} node.
   *
   * @param t The node traversal object that supplies context, such as the
   * scope chain to use in name lookups as well as error reporting.
   * @param n The node being visited.
   */
Method code:
1523 |   private void visitFunction(NodeTraversal t, Node n) {
1524 |     FunctionType functionType = JSType.toMaybeFunctionType(n.getJSType());
1525 |     String functionPrivateName = n.getFirstChild().getString();
1526 |     if (functionType.isConstructor()) {
1527 |       FunctionType baseConstructor = functionType.getSuperClassConstructor();
1528 |       if (baseConstructor != null &&
1529 |           baseConstructor != getNativeType(OBJECT_FUNCTION_TYPE) &&
1530 |           (baseConstructor.isInterface() && functionType.isConstructor())) {
1531 |         compiler.report(
1532 |             t.makeError(n, CONFLICTING_EXTENDED_TYPE, functionPrivateName));
1533 |       } else {
1534 |         // All interfaces are properly implemented by a class
1535 |         for (JSType baseInterface : functionType.getImplementedInterfaces()) {
1536 |           boolean badImplementedType = false;
1537 |           ObjectType baseInterfaceObj = ObjectType.cast(baseInterface);
1538 |           if (baseInterfaceObj != null) {
1539 |             FunctionType interfaceConstructor =
1540 |               baseInterfaceObj.getConstructor();
1541 |             if (interfaceConstructor != null &&
1542 |                 !interfaceConstructor.isInterface()) {
1543 |               badImplementedType = true;
1544 |             }
1545 |           } else {
1546 |             badImplementedType = true;
1547 |           }
1548 |           if (badImplementedType) {
1549 |             report(t, n, BAD_IMPLEMENTED_TYPE, functionPrivateName);
1550 |           }
1551 |         }
1552 |         // check properties
1553 |         validator.expectAllInterfaceProperties(t, n, functionType);
1554 |       }
1555 |     } else if (functionType.isInterface()) {
1556 |       // Interface must extend only interfaces
1557 |       for (ObjectType extInterface : functionType.getExtendedInterfaces()) {
1558 |         if (extInterface.getConstructor() != null
1559 |             && !extInterface.getConstructor().isInterface()) {
1560 |           compiler.report(
1561 |               t.makeError(n, CONFLICTING_EXTENDED_TYPE, functionPrivateName));
1562 |         }
1563 |       }
1564 |       // Interface cannot implement any interfaces
1565 |       if (functionType.hasImplementedInterfaces()) {
1566 |         compiler.report(t.makeError(n,
1567 |             CONFLICTING_IMPLEMENTED_TYPE, functionPrivateName));
1568 |       }
1569 |       // Check whether the extended interfaces have any conflicts
1570 |       if (functionType.getExtendedInterfacesCount() > 1) {
1571 |         // Only check when extending more than one interfaces
1572 |         HashMap<String, ObjectType> properties
1573 |             = new HashMap<String, ObjectType>();
1574 |         HashMap<String, ObjectType> currentProperties
1575 |             = new HashMap<String, ObjectType>();
1576 |         for (ObjectType interfaceType : functionType.getExtendedInterfaces()) {
1577 |           currentProperties.clear();
1578 |           checkInterfaceConflictProperties(t, n, functionPrivateName,
1579 |               properties, currentProperties, interfaceType);
1580 |           properties.putAll(currentProperties);
1581 |         }
1582 |       }
1583 |     }
1584 |   }

===== M0144 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypedScopeCreator.AbstractScopeBuilder.createFunctionTypeFromNodes(Node,String,JSDocInfo,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypedScopeCreator.java
Original range: 841-950
Documentation:
/**
     * Creates a new function type, based on the given nodes.
     *
     * This handles two cases that are semantically very different, but
     * are not mutually exclusive:
     * - A function literal that needs a type attached to it.
     * - An assignment expression with function-type info in the JsDoc.
     *
     * All parameters are optional, and we will do the best we can to create
     * a function type.
     *
     * This function will always create a function type, so only call it if
     * you're sure that's what you want.
     *
     * @param rValue The function node.
     * @param name the function's name
     * @param info the {@link JSDocInfo} attached to the function definition
     * @param lvalueNode The node where this function is being
     *     assigned. For example, {@code A.prototype.foo = ...} would be used to
     *     determine that this function is a method of A.prototype. May be
     *     null to indicate that this is not being assigned to a qualified name.
     */
Method code:
841 |     private FunctionType createFunctionTypeFromNodes(
842 |         @Nullable Node rValue,
843 |         @Nullable String name,
844 |         @Nullable JSDocInfo info,
845 |         @Nullable Node lvalueNode) {
846 | 
847 |       FunctionType functionType = null;
848 | 
849 |       // Global ctor aliases should be registered with the type registry.
850 |       if (rValue != null && rValue.isQualifiedName() && scope.isGlobal()) {
851 |         Var var = scope.getVar(rValue.getQualifiedName());
852 |         if (var != null && var.getType() != null &&
853 |             var.getType().isFunctionType()) {
854 |           FunctionType aliasedType  = var.getType().toMaybeFunctionType();
855 |           if ((aliasedType.isConstructor() || aliasedType.isInterface()) &&
856 |               !aliasedType.isNativeObjectType()) {
857 |             functionType = aliasedType;
858 | 
859 |             if (name != null && scope.isGlobal()) {
860 |               typeRegistry.declareType(name, functionType.getInstanceType());
861 |             }
862 |           }
863 |         }
864 |       }
865 | 
866 |       if (functionType == null) {
867 |         Node errorRoot = rValue == null ? lvalueNode : rValue;
868 |         boolean isFnLiteral =
869 |             rValue != null && rValue.isFunction();
870 |         Node fnRoot = isFnLiteral ? rValue : null;
871 |         Node parametersNode = isFnLiteral ?
872 |             rValue.getFirstChild().getNext() : null;
873 |         Node fnBlock = isFnLiteral ? parametersNode.getNext() : null;
874 | 
875 |         if (info != null && info.hasType()) {
876 |           JSType type = info.getType().evaluate(scope, typeRegistry);
877 | 
878 |           // Known to be not null since we have the FUNCTION token there.
879 |           type = type.restrictByNotNullOrUndefined();
880 |           if (type.isFunctionType()) {
881 |             functionType = type.toMaybeFunctionType();
882 |             functionType.setJSDocInfo(info);
883 |           }
884 |         }
885 | 
886 |         if (functionType == null) {
887 |           // Find the type of any overridden function.
888 |           Node ownerNode = NodeUtil.getBestLValueOwner(lvalueNode);
889 |           String ownerName = NodeUtil.getBestLValueName(ownerNode);
890 |           Var ownerVar = null;
891 |           String propName = null;
892 |           ObjectType ownerType = null;
893 |           if (ownerName != null) {
894 |             ownerVar = scope.getVar(ownerName);
895 |             if (ownerVar != null) {
896 |               ownerType = ObjectType.cast(ownerVar.getType());
897 |             }
898 |             if (name != null) {
899 |               propName = name.substring(ownerName.length() + 1);
900 |             }
901 |           }
902 | 
903 |           FunctionType overriddenPropType = null;
904 |           if (ownerType != null && propName != null) {
905 |             overriddenPropType =
906 |                 findOverriddenFunction(ownerType, propName);
907 |           }
908 | 
909 |           FunctionTypeBuilder builder =
910 |               new FunctionTypeBuilder(name, compiler, errorRoot, sourceName,
911 |                   scope)
912 |               .setContents(getFunctionAnalysisResults(fnRoot))
913 |               .inferFromOverriddenFunction(overriddenPropType, parametersNode)
914 |               .inferTemplateTypeName(info)
915 |               .inferReturnType(info)
916 |               .inferInheritance(info);
917 | 
918 | 
919 |           // Infer the context type.
920 |           boolean searchedForThisType = false;
921 |           if (ownerType != null && ownerType.isFunctionPrototypeType()) {
922 |             builder.inferThisType(
923 |                 info, ownerType.getOwnerFunction().getInstanceType());
924 |             searchedForThisType = true;
925 |           } else if (ownerNode != null && ownerNode.isThis()) {
926 |             // If 'this' has a type, use that instead.
927 |             // This is a hack, necessary because CollectProperties (below)
928 |             // doesn't run with the scope that it's building,
929 |             // so scope.getTypeOfThis() will be wrong.
930 |             JSType injectedThisType = ownerNode.getJSType();
931 |             builder.inferThisType(
932 |                 info,
933 |                 injectedThisType == null ?
934 |                 scope.getTypeOfThis() : injectedThisType);
935 |             searchedForThisType = true;
936 |           }
937 | 
938 |           if (!searchedForThisType) {
939 |             builder.inferThisType(info);
940 |           }
941 | 
942 |           functionType = builder
943 |               .inferParameterTypes(parametersNode, info)
944 |               .buildAndRegister();
945 |         }
946 |       }
947 | 
948 |       // all done
949 |       return functionType;
950 |     }

===== M0152 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypedScopeCreator.FirstOrderFunctionAnalyzer.visit(NodeTraversal,Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypedScopeCreator.java
Original range: 1974-2014
Documentation:

Method code:
1974 |     @Override public void visit(NodeTraversal t, Node n, Node parent) {
1975 |       if (t.inGlobalScope()) {
1976 |         return;
1977 |       }
1978 | 
1979 |       if (n.isReturn() && n.getFirstChild() != null) {
1980 |         data.get(t.getScopeRoot()).recordNonEmptyReturn();
1981 |       }
1982 | 
1983 |       if (t.getScopeDepth() <= 2) {
1984 |         // We only need to worry about escaped variables at depth 3.
1985 |         // An variable escaped at depth 2 is, by definition, a global variable.
1986 |         // We treat all global variables as escaped by default, so there's
1987 |         // no reason to do this extra computation for them.
1988 |         return;
1989 |       }
1990 | 
1991 |       if (n.isName() && NodeUtil.isLValue(n)) {
1992 |         String name = n.getString();
1993 |         Scope scope = t.getScope();
1994 |         Var var = scope.getVar(name);
1995 |         if (var != null) {
1996 |           Scope ownerScope = var.getScope();
1997 |           if (scope != ownerScope && ownerScope.isLocal()) {
1998 |             data.get(ownerScope.getRootNode()).recordEscapedVarName(name);
1999 |           }
2000 |         }
2001 |       } else if (n.isGetProp() && n.isUnscopedQualifiedName() &&
2002 |           NodeUtil.isLValue(n)) {
2003 |         String name = NodeUtil.getRootOfQualifiedName(n).getString();
2004 |         Scope scope = t.getScope();
2005 |         Var var = scope.getVar(name);
2006 |         if (var != null) {
2007 |           Scope ownerScope = var.getScope();
2008 |           if (scope != ownerScope && ownerScope.isLocal()) {
2009 |             data.get(ownerScope.getRootNode())
2010 |                 .recordEscapedQualifiedName(n.getQualifiedName());
2011 |           }
2012 |         }
2013 |       }
2014 |     }

Documentation:
/**
     * There are two types of calls we are interested in calls without explicit
     * "this" values (what we are call "free" calls) and direct call to eval.
     */
Method code:
164 |     private void annotateCalls(Node n) {
165 |       Preconditions.checkState(n.isCall());
166 | 
167 |       // Keep track of of the "this" context of a call.  A call without an
168 |       // explicit "this" is a free call.
169 |       Node first = n.getFirstChild();
170 |       if (!NodeUtil.isGet(first)) {
171 |         n.putBooleanProp(Node.FREE_CALL, true);
172 |       }
173 | 
174 |       // Keep track of the context in which eval is called. It is important
175 |       // to distinguish between "(0, eval)()" and "eval()".
176 |       if (first.isName() &&
177 |           "eval".equals(first.getString())) {
178 |         first.putBooleanProp(Node.DIRECT_EVAL, true);
179 |       }
180 |     }

/**
   * Declares a refined type in {@code scope} for the name represented by
   * {@code node}. It must be possible to refine the type of the given node in
   * the given scope, as determined by {@link #getTypeIfRefinable}.
   */
Method code:
157 |   protected void declareNameInScope(FlowScope scope, Node node, JSType type) {
158 |     switch (node.getType()) {
159 |       case Token.NAME:
160 |         scope.inferSlotType(node.getString(), type);
161 |         break;
162 | 
163 |       case Token.GETPROP:
164 |         String qualifiedName = node.getQualifiedName();
165 |         Preconditions.checkNotNull(qualifiedName);
166 | 
167 |         JSType origType = node.getJSType();
168 |         origType = origType == null ? getNativeType(UNKNOWN_TYPE) : origType;
169 |         scope.inferQualifiedSlot(node, qualifiedName, origType, type);
170 |         break;
171 | 
172 |       case Token.THIS:
173 |         // "this" references aren't currently modeled in the CFG.
174 |         break;
175 | 
176 |       default:

178 |             node.toStringTree());

===== M0167 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.DataFlowAnalysis.analyze(int)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/DataFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 201-229
Documentation:
/**
   * Finds a fixed-point solution. The function has the side effect of replacing
   * the existing node annotations with the computed solutions using {@link
   * com.google.javascript.jscomp.graph.GraphNode#setAnnotation(Annotation)}.
   *
   * <p>Initially, each node's input and output flow state contains the value
   * given by {@link #createInitialEstimateLattice()} (with the exception of the
   * entry node of the graph which takes on the {@link #createEntryLattice()}
   * value. Each node will use the output state of its predecessor and compute a
   * output state according to the instruction. At that time, any nodes that
   * depends on the node's newly modified output value will need to recompute
   * their output state again. Each step will perform a computation at one node
   * until no extra computation will modify any existing output state anymore.
   *
   * @param maxSteps Max number of iterations before the method stops and throw
   *        a {@link MaxIterationsExceededException}. This will prevent the
   *        analysis from going into a infinite loop.
   */
Method code:
201 |   final void analyze(int maxSteps) {
202 |     initialize();
203 |     int step = 0;
204 |     while (!orderedWorkSet.isEmpty()) {
205 |       if (step > maxSteps) {
206 |         throw new MaxIterationsExceededException(
207 |           "Analysis did not terminate after " + maxSteps + " iterations");
208 |       }
209 |       DiGraphNode<N, Branch> curNode = orderedWorkSet.iterator().next();
210 |       orderedWorkSet.remove(curNode);
211 |       joinInputs(curNode);
212 |       if (flow(curNode)) {
213 |         // If there is a change in the current node, we want to grab the list
214 |         // of nodes that this node affects.
215 |         List<DiGraphNode<N, Branch>> nextNodes = isForward() ?
216 |             cfg.getDirectedSuccNodes(curNode) :
217 |             cfg.getDirectedPredNodes(curNode);
218 |         for (DiGraphNode<N, Branch> nextNode : nextNodes) {
219 |           if (nextNode != cfg.getImplicitReturn()) {
220 |             orderedWorkSet.add(nextNode);
221 |           }
222 |         }
223 |       }
224 |       step++;
225 |     }
226 |     if (isForward()) {
227 |       joinInputs(getCfg().getImplicitReturn());
228 |     }
229 |   }

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypedScopeCreator.AbstractScopeBuilder.checkForClassDefiningCalls(NodeTraversal,Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypedScopeCreator.java
Original range: 1343-1415
Documentation:
/**
     * Look for class-defining calls.
     * Because JS has no 'native' syntax for defining classes,
     * this is often very coding-convention dependent and business-logic heavy.
     */
Method code:
1343 |     private void checkForClassDefiningCalls(
1344 |         NodeTraversal t, Node n, Node parent) {
1345 |       SubclassRelationship relationship =
1346 |           codingConvention.getClassesDefinedByCall(n);
1347 |       if (relationship != null) {
1348 |         FunctionType superCtor = getFunctionType(
1349 |             scope.getVar(relationship.superclassName));
1350 |         FunctionType subCtor = getFunctionType(
1351 |             scope.getVar(relationship.subclassName));
1352 |         if (superCtor != null && superCtor.isConstructor() &&
1353 |             subCtor != null && subCtor.isConstructor()) {
1354 |           ObjectType superClass = superCtor.getInstanceType();
1355 |           ObjectType subClass = subCtor.getInstanceType();
1356 | 
1357 |           // superCtor and subCtor might be structural constructors
1358 |           // (like {function(new:Object)}) so we need to resolve them back
1359 |           // to the original ctor objects.
1360 |           superCtor = superClass.getConstructor();
1361 |           subCtor = subClass.getConstructor();
1362 | 
1363 |           if (relationship.type == SubclassType.INHERITS &&
1364 |               !superClass.isEmptyType() && !subClass.isEmptyType()) {
1365 |             validator.expectSuperType(t, n, superClass, subClass);
1366 |           }
1367 | 
1368 |           if (superCtor != null && subCtor != null) {
1369 |             codingConvention.applySubclassRelationship(
1370 |                 superCtor, subCtor, relationship.type);
1371 |           }
1372 |         }
1373 |       }
1374 | 
1375 |       String singletonGetterClassName =
1376 |           codingConvention.getSingletonGetterClassName(n);
1377 |       if (singletonGetterClassName != null) {
1378 |         ObjectType objectType = ObjectType.cast(
1379 |             typeRegistry.getType(singletonGetterClassName));
1380 |         if (objectType != null) {
1381 |           FunctionType functionType = objectType.getConstructor();
1382 | 
1383 |           if (functionType != null) {
1384 |             FunctionType getterType =
1385 |                 typeRegistry.createFunctionType(objectType);
1386 |             codingConvention.applySingletonGetter(functionType, getterType,
1387 |                 objectType);
1388 |           }
1389 |         }
1390 |       }
1391 | 
1392 |       DelegateRelationship delegateRelationship =
1393 |           codingConvention.getDelegateRelationship(n);
1394 |       if (delegateRelationship != null) {
1395 |         applyDelegateRelationship(delegateRelationship);
1396 |       }
1397 | 
1398 |       ObjectLiteralCast objectLiteralCast =
1399 |           codingConvention.getObjectLiteralCast(n);
1400 |       if (objectLiteralCast != null) {
1401 |         if (objectLiteralCast.diagnosticType == null) {
1402 |           ObjectType type = ObjectType.cast(
1403 |               typeRegistry.getType(objectLiteralCast.typeName));
1404 |           if (type != null && type.getConstructor() != null) {
1405 |             setDeferredType(objectLiteralCast.objectNode, type);
1406 |           } else {
1407 |             compiler.report(JSError.make(t.getSourceName(), n,
1408 |                     CONSTRUCTOR_EXPECTED));
1409 |           }
1410 |         } else {
1411 |           compiler.report(JSError.make(t.getSourceName(), n,
1412 |                   objectLiteralCast.diagnosticType));
1413 |         }
1414 |       }
1415 |     }

===== M0185 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowGraph.isEnteringNewCfgNode(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowGraph.java
Original range: 159-205
Documentation:
/**
   * @return True if n should be represented by a new CFG node in the control
   * flow graph.
   */
Method code:
159 |   public static boolean isEnteringNewCfgNode(Node n) {
160 |     Node parent = n.getParent();
161 |     switch (parent.getType()) {
162 |       case Token.BLOCK:
163 |       case Token.SCRIPT:
164 |       case Token.TRY:
165 |         return true;
166 |       case Token.FUNCTION:
167 |         // A function node represents the start of a function where the name
168 |         // is bleed into the local scope and parameters has been assigned
169 |         // to the formal argument names. The node includes the name of the
170 |         // function and the LP list since we assume the whole set up process
171 |         // is atomic without change in control flow. The next change of
172 |         // control is going into the function's body represent by the second
173 |         // child.
174 |         return n != parent.getFirstChild().getNext();
175 |       case Token.WHILE:
176 |       case Token.DO:
177 |       case Token.IF:
178 |         // Theses control structure is represented by its node that holds the
179 |         // condition. Each of them is a branch node based on its condition.
180 |         return NodeUtil.getConditionExpression(parent) != n;
181 | 
182 |       case Token.FOR:
183 |         // The FOR(;;) node differs from other control structure in that
184 |         // it has a initialization and a increment statement. Those
185 |         // two statements have its corresponding CFG nodes to represent them.
186 |         // The FOR node represents the condition check for each iteration.
187 |         // That way the following:
188 |         // for(var x = 0; x < 10; x++) { } has a graph that is isomorphic to
189 |         // var x = 0; while(x<10) {  x++; }
190 |         if (NodeUtil.isForIn(parent)) {
191 |           // TODO(user): Investigate how we should handle the case where
192 |           // we have a very complex expression inside the FOR-IN header.
193 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild();
194 |         } else {
195 |           return NodeUtil.getConditionExpression(parent) != n;
196 |         }
197 |       case Token.SWITCH:
198 |       case Token.CASE:
199 |       case Token.CATCH:
200 |       case Token.WITH:
201 |         return n != parent.getFirstChild();
202 |       default:
203 |         return false;
204 |     }
205 |   }

248 |   private FlowScope traverse(Node n, FlowScope scope) {
249 |     switch (n.getType()) {
250 |       case Token.ASSIGN:
251 |         scope = traverseAssign(n, scope);
252 |         break;
253 | 
254 |       case Token.NAME:
255 |         scope = traverseName(n, scope);
256 |         break;
257 | 
258 |       case Token.GETPROP:
259 |         scope = traverseGetProp(n, scope);
260 |         break;
261 | 
262 |       case Token.AND:
263 |         scope = traverseAnd(n, scope).getJoinedFlowScope()
264 |             .createChildFlowScope();
265 |         break;
266 | 
267 |       case Token.OR:
268 |         scope = traverseOr(n, scope).getJoinedFlowScope()
269 |             .createChildFlowScope();
270 |         break;
271 | 
272 |       case Token.HOOK:
273 |         scope = traverseHook(n, scope);
274 |         break;
275 | 
276 |       case Token.OBJECTLIT:
277 |         scope = traverseObjectLiteral(n, scope);
278 |         break;
279 | 
280 |       case Token.CALL:
281 |         scope = traverseCall(n, scope);
282 |         break;
283 | 
284 |       case Token.NEW:
285 |         scope = traverseNew(n, scope);
286 |         break;
287 | 
288 |       case Token.ASSIGN_ADD:
289 |       case Token.ADD:
290 |         scope = traverseAdd(n, scope);
291 |         break;
292 | 
293 |       case Token.POS:
294 |       case Token.NEG:
295 |         scope = traverse(n.getFirstChild(), scope);  // Find types.
296 |         n.setJSType(getNativeType(NUMBER_TYPE));
297 |         break;
298 | 
299 |       case Token.ARRAYLIT:
300 |         scope = traverseArrayLiteral(n, scope);
301 |         break;
302 | 
303 |       case Token.THIS:
304 |         n.setJSType(scope.getTypeOfThis());
305 |         break;
306 | 
307 |       case Token.ASSIGN_LSH:
308 |       case Token.ASSIGN_RSH:
309 |       case Token.LSH:
310 |       case Token.RSH:
311 |       case Token.ASSIGN_URSH:
312 |       case Token.URSH:
313 |       case Token.ASSIGN_DIV:
314 |       case Token.ASSIGN_MOD:
315 |       case Token.ASSIGN_BITAND:
316 |       case Token.ASSIGN_BITXOR:
317 |       case Token.ASSIGN_BITOR:
318 |       case Token.ASSIGN_MUL:
319 |       case Token.ASSIGN_SUB:
320 |       case Token.DIV:
321 |       case Token.MOD:
322 |       case Token.BITAND:
323 |       case Token.BITXOR:
324 |       case Token.BITOR:
325 |       case Token.MUL:
326 |       case Token.SUB:
327 |       case Token.DEC:
328 |       case Token.INC:
329 |       case Token.BITNOT:
330 |         scope = traverseChildren(n, scope);
331 |         n.setJSType(getNativeType(NUMBER_TYPE));
332 |         break;
333 |

335 |         scope = traverse(n.getFirstChild(), scope);
336 |         n.setJSType(getJSType(n.getFirstChild()));
337 |         break;
338 | 
339 |       case Token.COMMA:
340 |         scope = traverseChildren(n, scope);
341 |         n.setJSType(getJSType(n.getLastChild()));
342 |         break;
343 | 
344 |       case Token.TYPEOF:
345 |         scope = traverseChildren(n, scope);
346 |         n.setJSType(getNativeType(STRING_TYPE));
347 |         break;
348 | 
349 |       case Token.DELPROP:
350 |       case Token.LT:
351 |       case Token.LE:
352 |       case Token.GT:
353 |       case Token.GE:
354 |       case Token.NOT:
355 |       case Token.EQ:
356 |       case Token.NE:
357 |       case Token.SHEQ:
358 |       case Token.SHNE:
359 |       case Token.INSTANCEOF:
360 |       case Token.IN:
361 |         scope = traverseChildren(n, scope);
362 |         n.setJSType(getNativeType(BOOLEAN_TYPE));
363 |         break;

366 |         scope = traverseGetElem(n, scope);
367 |         break;
368 |

370 |         scope = traverseChildren(n, scope);
371 |         if (n.getFirstChild().isGetProp()) {
372 |           ensurePropertyDeclared(n.getFirstChild());
373 |         }
374 |         break;
375 | 
376 |       case Token.SWITCH:
377 |         scope = traverse(n.getFirstChild(), scope);
378 |         break;
379 | 
380 |       case Token.RETURN:
381 |         scope = traverseReturn(n, scope);
382 |         break;
383 | 
384 |       case Token.VAR:
385 |       case Token.THROW:
386 |         scope = traverseChildren(n, scope);
387 |         break;
388 | 
389 |       case Token.CATCH:
390 |         scope = traverseCatch(n, scope);
391 |         break;
392 |     }
393 |     if (!n.isFunction()) {
394 |       JSDocInfo info = n.getJSDocInfo();
395 |       if (info != null && info.hasType()) {
396 |         JSType castType = info.getType().evaluate(syntacticScope, registry);
397 | 
398 |         // A stubbed type cast on a qualified name should take
399 |         // effect for all subsequent accesses of that name,
400 |         // so treat it the same as an assign to that name.
401 |         if (n.isQualifiedName() &&
402 |             n.getParent().isExprResult()) {
403 |           updateScopeForTypeChange(scope, n, n.getJSType(), castType);
404 |         }
405 | 
406 |         n.setJSType(castType);
407 |       }
408 |     }
409 | 
410 |     return scope;
411 |   }

3090 |   static String getBestLValueName(@Nullable Node lValue) {

3092 |       return null;

372 |       this.moduleGraph = null;
373 |     }

377 |

/**
   * Resolve this type in the given scope.
   *
   * The returned value must be equal to {@code this}, as defined by
   * {@link #isEquivalentTo}. It may or may not be the same object. This method
   * may modify the internal state of {@code this}, as long as it does
   * so in a way that preserves Object equality.
   *
   * For efficiency, we should only resolve a type once per compilation job.
   * For incremental compilations, one compilation job may need the
   * artifacts from a previous generation, so we will eventually need
   * a generational flag instead of a boolean one.
   */
Method code:
1140 |   public final JSType resolve(ErrorReporter t, StaticScope<JSType> scope) {
1141 |     if (resolved) {
1142 |       // TODO(nicksantos): Check to see if resolve() looped back on itself.
1143 |       // Preconditions.checkNotNull(resolveResult);
1144 |       if (resolveResult == null) {
1145 |         return registry.getNativeType(JSTypeNative.UNKNOWN_TYPE);
1146 |       }
1147 |       return resolveResult;
1148 |     }
1149 |     resolved = true;
1150 |     resolveResult = resolveInternal(t, scope);
1151 |     resolveResult.setResolvedTypeInternal(resolveResult);
1152 |     return resolveResult;
1153 |   }

/**
   * Visits a VAR node.
   *
   * @param t The node traversal object that supplies context, such as the
   * scope chain to use in name lookups as well as error reporting.
   * @param n The node being visited.
   */
Method code:
1429 |   private void visitVar(NodeTraversal t, Node n) {
1430 |     // TODO(nicksantos): Fix this so that the doc info always shows up
1431 |     // on the NAME node. We probably want to wait for the parser
1432 |     // merge to fix this.
1433 |     JSDocInfo varInfo = n.hasOneChild() ? n.getJSDocInfo() : null;
1434 |     for (Node name : n.children()) {
1435 |       Node value = name.getFirstChild();
1436 |       // A null var would indicate a bug in the scope creation logic.
1437 |       Var var = t.getScope().getVar(name.getString());
1438 | 
1439 |       if (value != null) {
1440 |         JSType valueType = getJSType(value);
1441 |         JSType nameType = var.getType();
1442 |         nameType = (nameType == null) ? getNativeType(UNKNOWN_TYPE) : nameType;
1443 | 
1444 |         JSDocInfo info = name.getJSDocInfo();
1445 |         if (info == null) {
1446 |           info = varInfo;
1447 |         }
1448 | 
1449 |         checkEnumAlias(t, info, value);
1450 |         if (var.isTypeInferred()) {
1451 |           ensureTyped(t, name, valueType);
1452 |         } else {
1453 |           validator.expectCanAssignTo(
1454 |               t, value, valueType, nameType, "initializing variable");
1455 |         }
1456 |       }
1457 |     }
1458 |   }

===== M0211 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.NodeTraversal.traverseFunction(Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/NodeTraversal.java
Original range: 510-545
Documentation:
/**
   * Traverses a function.
   */
Method code:
510 |   private void traverseFunction(Node n, Node parent) {
511 |     Preconditions.checkState(n.getChildCount() == 3);
512 |     Preconditions.checkState(n.isFunction());
513 | 
514 |     final Node fnName = n.getFirstChild();
515 | 
516 |     boolean isFunctionExpression = (parent != null)
517 |         && NodeUtil.isFunctionExpression(n);
518 | 
519 |     if (!isFunctionExpression) {
520 |       // Functions declarations are in the scope containing the declaration.
521 |       traverseBranch(fnName, n);
522 |     }
523 | 
524 |     curNode = n;
525 |     pushScope(n);
526 | 
527 |     if (isFunctionExpression) {
528 |       // Function expression names are only accessible within the function
529 |       // scope.
530 |       traverseBranch(fnName, n);
531 |     }
532 | 
533 |     final Node args = fnName.getNext();
534 |     final Node body = args.getNext();
535 | 
536 |     // Args
537 |     traverseBranch(args, n);
538 | 
539 |     // Body
540 |     Preconditions.checkState(body.getNext() == null &&
541 |             body.isBlock(), body);
542 |     traverseBranch(body, n);
543 | 
544 |     popScope();
545 |   }

===== M0218 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeCheck.visit(NodeTraversal,Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeCheck.java
Original range: 461-810
Documentation:
/**
   * This is the meat of the type checking.  It is basically one big switch,
   * with each case representing one type of parse tree node.  The individual
   * cases are usually pretty straightforward.
   *
   * @param t The node traversal object that supplies context, such as the
   * scope chain to use in name lookups as well as error reporting.
   * @param n The node being visited.
   * @param parent The parent of the node n.
   */
Method code:
461 |   @Override
462 |   public void visit(NodeTraversal t, Node n, Node parent) {
463 |     JSType childType;
464 |     JSType leftType, rightType;
465 |     Node left, right;
466 |     // To be explicitly set to false if the node is not typeable.
467 |     boolean typeable = true;
468 | 
469 |     switch (n.getType()) {
470 |       case Token.NAME:
471 |         typeable = visitName(t, n, parent);
472 |         break;
473 | 
474 |       case Token.PARAM_LIST:
475 |         // If this is under a FUNCTION node, it is a parameter list and can be
476 |         // ignored here.
477 |         if (!parent.isFunction()) {
478 |           ensureTyped(t, n, getJSType(n.getFirstChild()));
479 |         } else {
480 |           typeable = false;
481 |         }
482 |         break;
483 | 
484 |       case Token.COMMA:
485 |         ensureTyped(t, n, getJSType(n.getLastChild()));
486 |         break;
487 | 
488 |       case Token.TRUE:
489 |       case Token.FALSE:
490 |         ensureTyped(t, n, BOOLEAN_TYPE);
491 |         break;
492 | 
493 |       case Token.THIS:
494 |         ensureTyped(t, n, t.getScope().getTypeOfThis());
495 |         break;
496 | 
497 |       case Token.NULL:
498 |         ensureTyped(t, n, NULL_TYPE);
499 |         break;
500 | 
501 |       case Token.NUMBER:
502 |         ensureTyped(t, n, NUMBER_TYPE);
503 |         break;
504 | 
505 |       case Token.STRING:
506 |         ensureTyped(t, n, STRING_TYPE);
507 |         break;
508 | 
509 |       case Token.STRING_KEY:
510 |         typeable = false;
511 |         break;
512 | 
513 |       case Token.GETTER_DEF:
514 |       case Token.SETTER_DEF:
515 |         // Object literal keys are handled with OBJECTLIT
516 |         break;
517 | 
518 |       case Token.ARRAYLIT:
519 |         ensureTyped(t, n, ARRAY_TYPE);
520 |         break;
521 | 
522 |       case Token.REGEXP:
523 |         ensureTyped(t, n, REGEXP_TYPE);
524 |         break;
525 | 
526 |       case Token.GETPROP:
527 |         visitGetProp(t, n, parent);
528 |         typeable = !(parent.isAssign() &&
529 |                      parent.getFirstChild() == n);
530 |         break;
531 | 
532 |       case Token.GETELEM:
533 |         visitGetElem(t, n);
534 |         // The type of GETELEM is always unknown, so no point counting that.
535 |         // If that unknown leaks elsewhere (say by an assignment to another
536 |         // variable), then it will be counted.
537 |         typeable = false;
538 |         break;
539 | 
540 |       case Token.VAR:
541 |         visitVar(t, n);
542 |         typeable = false;
543 |         break;
544 | 
545 |       case Token.NEW:
546 |         visitNew(t, n);
547 |         typeable = true;
548 |         break;
549 | 
550 |       case Token.CALL:
551 |         visitCall(t, n);
552 |         typeable = !parent.isExprResult();
553 |         break;
554 | 
555 |       case Token.RETURN:
556 |         visitReturn(t, n);
557 |         typeable = false;
558 |         break;
559 | 
560 |       case Token.DEC:
561 |       case Token.INC:
562 |         left = n.getFirstChild();
563 |         validator.expectNumber(
564 |             t, left, getJSType(left), "increment/decrement");
565 |         ensureTyped(t, n, NUMBER_TYPE);
566 |         break;
567 | 
568 |       case Token.NOT:
569 |         ensureTyped(t, n, BOOLEAN_TYPE);
570 |         break;
571 | 
572 |       case Token.VOID:
573 |         ensureTyped(t, n, VOID_TYPE);
574 |         break;
575 | 
576 |       case Token.TYPEOF:
577 |         ensureTyped(t, n, STRING_TYPE);
578 |         break;
579 | 
580 |       case Token.BITNOT:
581 |         childType = getJSType(n.getFirstChild());
582 |         if (!childType.matchesInt32Context()) {
583 |           report(t, n, BIT_OPERATION, NodeUtil.opToStr(n.getType()),
584 |               childType.toString());
585 |         }
586 |         ensureTyped(t, n, NUMBER_TYPE);
587 |         break;
588 | 
589 |       case Token.POS:
590 |       case Token.NEG:
591 |         left = n.getFirstChild();
592 |         validator.expectNumber(t, left, getJSType(left), "sign operator");
593 |         ensureTyped(t, n, NUMBER_TYPE);
594 |         break;
595 | 
596 |       case Token.EQ:
597 |       case Token.NE:
598 |       case Token.SHEQ:
599 |       case Token.SHNE: {
600 |         leftType = getJSType(n.getFirstChild());
601 |         rightType = getJSType(n.getLastChild());
602 | 
603 |         // We do not want to warn about explicit comparisons to VOID. People
604 |         // often do this if they think their type annotations screwed up.
605 |         //
606 |         // We do want to warn about cases where people compare things like
607 |         // (Array|null) == (Function|null)
608 |         // because it probably means they screwed up.
609 |         //
610 |         // This heuristic here is not perfect, but should catch cases we
611 |         // care about without too many false negatives.
612 |         JSType leftTypeRestricted = leftType.restrictByNotNullOrUndefined();
613 |         JSType rightTypeRestricted = rightType.restrictByNotNullOrUndefined();
614 | 
615 |         TernaryValue result = TernaryValue.UNKNOWN;
616 |         if (n.getType() == Token.EQ || n.getType() == Token.NE) {
617 |           result = leftTypeRestricted.testForEquality(
618 |               rightTypeRestricted);
619 |           if (n.isNE()) {
620 |             result = result.not();
621 |           }
622 |         } else {
623 |           // SHEQ or SHNE
624 |           if (!leftTypeRestricted.canTestForShallowEqualityWith(
625 |                   rightTypeRestricted)) {
626 |             result = n.getType() == Token.SHEQ ?
627 |                 TernaryValue.FALSE : TernaryValue.TRUE;
628 |           }
629 |         }
630 | 
631 |         if (result != TernaryValue.UNKNOWN) {
632 |           report(t, n, DETERMINISTIC_TEST, leftType.toString(),
633 |               rightType.toString(), result.toString());
634 |         }
635 |         ensureTyped(t, n, BOOLEAN_TYPE);
636 |         break;
637 |       }
638 | 
639 |       case Token.LT:
640 |       case Token.LE:
641 |       case Token.GT:
642 |       case Token.GE:
643 |         leftType = getJSType(n.getFirstChild());
644 |         rightType = getJSType(n.getLastChild());
645 |         if (rightType.isNumber()) {
646 |           validator.expectNumber(
647 |               t, n, leftType, "left side of numeric comparison");
648 |         } else if (leftType.isNumber()) {
649 |           validator.expectNumber(
650 |               t, n, rightType, "right side of numeric comparison");
651 |         } else if (leftType.matchesNumberContext() &&
652 |                    rightType.matchesNumberContext()) {
653 |           // OK.
654 |         } else {
655 |           // Whether the comparison is numeric will be determined at runtime
656 |           // each time the expression is evaluated. Regardless, both operands
657 |           // should match a string context.
658 |           String message = "left side of comparison";
659 |           validator.expectString(t, n, leftType, message);
660 |           validator.expectNotNullOrUndefined(
661 |               t, n, leftType, message, getNativeType(STRING_TYPE));
662 |           message = "right side of comparison";
663 |           validator.expectString(t, n, rightType, message);
664 |           validator.expectNotNullOrUndefined(
665 |               t, n, rightType, message, getNativeType(STRING_TYPE));
666 |         }
667 |         ensureTyped(t, n, BOOLEAN_TYPE);
668 |         break;
669 | 
670 |       case Token.IN:
671 |         left = n.getFirstChild();
672 |         right = n.getLastChild();
673 |         leftType = getJSType(left);
674 |         rightType = getJSType(right);
675 |         validator.expectObject(t, n, rightType, "'in' requires an object");
676 |         validator.expectString(t, left, leftType, "left side of 'in'");
677 |         ensureTyped(t, n, BOOLEAN_TYPE);
678 |         break;
679 | 
680 |       case Token.INSTANCEOF:
681 |         left = n.getFirstChild();
682 |         right = n.getLastChild();
683 |         leftType = getJSType(left);
684 |         rightType = getJSType(right).restrictByNotNullOrUndefined();
685 | 
686 |         validator.expectAnyObject(
687 |             t, left, leftType, "deterministic instanceof yields false");
688 |         validator.expectActualObject(
689 |             t, right, rightType, "instanceof requires an object");
690 |         ensureTyped(t, n, BOOLEAN_TYPE);
691 |         break;
692 | 
693 |       case Token.ASSIGN:
694 |         visitAssign(t, n);
695 |         typeable = false;
696 |         break;
697 | 
698 |       case Token.ASSIGN_LSH:
699 |       case Token.ASSIGN_RSH:
700 |       case Token.ASSIGN_URSH:
701 |       case Token.ASSIGN_DIV:
702 |       case Token.ASSIGN_MOD:
703 |       case Token.ASSIGN_BITOR:
704 |       case Token.ASSIGN_BITXOR:
705 |       case Token.ASSIGN_BITAND:
706 |       case Token.ASSIGN_SUB:
707 |       case Token.ASSIGN_ADD:
708 |       case Token.ASSIGN_MUL:
709 |       case Token.LSH:
710 |       case Token.RSH:
711 |       case Token.URSH:
712 |       case Token.DIV:
713 |       case Token.MOD:
714 |       case Token.BITOR:
715 |       case Token.BITXOR:
716 |       case Token.BITAND:
717 |       case Token.SUB:
718 |       case Token.ADD:
719 |       case Token.MUL:
720 |         visitBinaryOperator(n.getType(), t, n);
721 |         break;
722 | 
723 |       case Token.DELPROP:
724 |         ensureTyped(t, n, BOOLEAN_TYPE);
725 |         break;
726 | 
727 |       case Token.CASE:
728 |         JSType switchType = getJSType(parent.getFirstChild());
729 |         JSType caseType = getJSType(n.getFirstChild());
730 |         validator.expectSwitchMatchesCase(t, n, switchType, caseType);
731 |         typeable = false;
732 |         break;
733 | 
734 |       case Token.WITH: {
735 |         Node child = n.getFirstChild();
736 |         childType = getJSType(child);
737 |         validator.expectObject(
738 |             t, child, childType, "with requires an object");
739 |         typeable = false;
740 |         break;
741 |       }
742 | 
743 |       case Token.FUNCTION:
744 |         visitFunction(t, n);
745 |         break;
746 | 
747 |       // These nodes have no interesting type behavior.
748 |       case Token.LABEL:
749 |       case Token.LABEL_NAME:
750 |       case Token.SWITCH:
751 |       case Token.BREAK:
752 |       case Token.CATCH:
753 |       case Token.TRY:
754 |       case Token.SCRIPT:
755 |       case Token.EXPR_RESULT:
756 |       case Token.BLOCK:
757 |       case Token.EMPTY:
758 |       case Token.DEFAULT_CASE:
759 |       case Token.CONTINUE:
760 |       case Token.DEBUGGER:
761 |       case Token.THROW:
762 |         typeable = false;
763 |         break;
764 | 
765 |       // These nodes require data flow analysis.
766 |       case Token.DO:
767 |       case Token.FOR:
768 |       case Token.IF:
769 |       case Token.WHILE:
770 |         typeable = false;
771 |         break;
772 | 
773 |       // These nodes are typed during the type inference.
774 |       case Token.AND:
775 |       case Token.HOOK:
776 |       case Token.OBJECTLIT:
777 |       case Token.OR:
778 |         if (n.getJSType() != null) { // If we didn't run type inference.
779 |           ensureTyped(t, n);
780 |         } else {
781 |           // If this is an enum, then give that type to the objectlit as well.
782 |           if ((n.isObjectLit())
783 |               && (parent.getJSType() instanceof EnumType)) {
784 |             ensureTyped(t, n, parent.getJSType());
785 |           } else {
786 |             ensureTyped(t, n);
787 |           }
788 |         }
789 |         if (n.isObjectLit()) {
790 |           for (Node key : n.children()) {
791 |             visitObjLitKey(t, key, n);
792 |           }
793 |         }
794 |         break;
795 | 
796 |       default:
797 |         report(t, n, UNEXPECTED_TOKEN, Token.name(n.getType()));
798 |         ensureTyped(t, n);
799 |         break;
800 |     }
801 | 
802 |     // Don't count externs since the user's code may not even use that part.
803 |     typeable = typeable && !inExterns;
804 | 
805 |     if (typeable) {
806 |       doPercentTypedAccounting(t, n);
807 |     }
808 | 
809 |     checkNoTypeCheckSection(n, false);
810 |   }

/**
   * Returns the type of a node in the given scope if the node corresponds to a
   * name whose type is capable of being refined.
   * @return The current type of the node if it can be refined, null otherwise.
   */
Method code:
118 |   protected JSType getTypeIfRefinable(Node node, FlowScope scope) {
119 |     switch (node.getType()) {
120 |       case Token.NAME:
121 |         StaticSlot<JSType> nameVar = scope.getSlot(node.getString());
122 |         if (nameVar != null) {
123 |           JSType nameVarType = nameVar.getType();
124 |           if (nameVarType == null) {
125 |             nameVarType = node.getJSType();
126 |           }
127 |           return nameVarType;
128 |         }
129 |         return null;
130 | 
131 |       case Token.GETPROP:
132 |         String qualifiedName = node.getQualifiedName();
133 |         if (qualifiedName == null) {
134 |           return null;
135 |         }
136 |         StaticSlot<JSType> propVar = scope.getSlot(qualifiedName);
137 |         JSType propVarType = null;
138 |         if (propVar != null) {
139 |           propVarType = propVar.getType();
140 |         }
141 |         if (propVarType == null) {
142 |           propVarType = node.getJSType();
143 |         }
144 |         if (propVarType == null) {
145 |           propVarType = getNativeType(UNKNOWN_TYPE);
146 |         }
147 |         return propVarType;
148 |     }
149 |     return null;
150 |   }

203 |   private boolean isConnectedInDirection(N n1, Predicate<E> edgeMatcher, N n2) {
204 |     // Verify the nodes.
205 |     DiGraphNode<N, E> dNode1 = getNodeOrFail(n1);
206 |     DiGraphNode<N, E> dNode2 = getNodeOrFail(n2);

===== M0229 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ClosureCodingConvention.getClassesDefinedByCall(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ClosureCodingConvention.java
Original range: 94-153
Documentation:
/**
   * {@inheritDoc}
   *
   * <p>Understands several different inheritance patterns that occur in
   * Google code (various uses of {@code inherits} and {@code mixin}).
   */
Method code:
94 |   @Override
95 |   public SubclassRelationship getClassesDefinedByCall(Node callNode) {
96 |     SubclassRelationship relationship =
97 |         super.getClassesDefinedByCall(callNode);
98 |     if (relationship != null) return relationship;
99 | 
100 |     Node callName = callNode.getFirstChild();
101 |     SubclassType type = typeofClassDefiningName(callName);
102 |     if (type != null) {
103 |       Node subclass = null;
104 |       Node superclass = callNode.getLastChild();
105 | 
106 |       // There are six possible syntaxes for a class-defining method:
107 |       // SubClass.inherits(SuperClass)
108 |       // goog.inherits(SubClass, SuperClass)
109 |       // goog$inherits(SubClass, SuperClass)
110 |       // SubClass.mixin(SuperClass.prototype)
111 |       // goog.mixin(SubClass.prototype, SuperClass.prototype)
112 |       // goog$mixin(SubClass.prototype, SuperClass.prototype)
113 |       boolean isDeprecatedCall = callNode.getChildCount() == 2 &&
114 |           callName.isGetProp();
115 |       if (isDeprecatedCall) {
116 |         // SubClass.inherits(SuperClass)
117 |         subclass = callName.getFirstChild();
118 |       } else if (callNode.getChildCount() == 3) {
119 |         // goog.inherits(SubClass, SuperClass)
120 |         subclass = callName.getNext();
121 |       } else {
122 |         return null;
123 |       }
124 | 
125 |       if (type == SubclassType.MIXIN) {
126 |         // Only consider mixins that mix two prototypes as related to
127 |         // inheritance.
128 |         if (!endsWithPrototype(superclass)) {
129 |           return null;
130 |         }
131 |         if (!isDeprecatedCall) {
132 |           if (!endsWithPrototype(subclass)) {
133 |             return null;
134 |           }
135 |           // Strip off the prototype from the name.
136 |           subclass = subclass.getFirstChild();
137 |         }
138 |         superclass = superclass.getFirstChild();
139 |       }
140 | 
141 |       // bail out if either of the side of the "inherits"
142 |       // isn't a real class name. This prevents us from
143 |       // doing something weird in cases like:
144 |       // goog.inherits(MySubClass, cond ? SuperClass1 : BaseClass2)
145 |       if (subclass != null &&
146 |           subclass.isUnscopedQualifiedName() &&
147 |           superclass.isUnscopedQualifiedName()) {
148 |         return new SubclassRelationship(type, subclass, superclass);
149 |       }
150 |     }
151 | 
152 |     return null;
153 |   }
